## Document 008: 10月美国就业数据点评：就业市场反弹，紧缩预期延续

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月美国就业数据点评：就业市场反弹，紧缩预期延续.pdf
Report source: 爱建证券
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告
总量研究 / 宏观研究 2026 年 11 月 09 日
细分研究
就业市场反弹，紧缩预期延续
——10 月美国就业数据点评
相关研究
-
投资要点：
 事件：11 月 6 日美国劳工部公布就业数据显示，10 月美国新增非农就业 16.5 万人，失业率
为 4.1%，较上月改善。
 核心观点：10 月美国就业市场明显反弹，新增岗位大幅增加，失业率小幅回落，劳动力市场
韧性有所增强。（1）总体看，新增非农就业明显回升，失业率小幅下降，就业市场短期有所
修复。（2）结构看，服务业为新增就业主要支撑，耐用品制造业表现较好，但部分传统服务
行业就业偏弱，行业分化仍存。（3）工资看，薪资同比增速小幅放缓，环比增速有所回升，
整体工资上涨压力相对温和。（4）政策预期看，就业市场反弹降低了美联储对劳动力市场走
弱的担忧，叠加通胀仍处高位，美联储加息必要性仍存，但油价回落或推动紧缩节奏放缓。
 总体：新增就业明显反弹，失业压力有所缓解。
 美国新增非农就业明显回升，失业率小幅下降，劳动力市场扩张动能有所恢复。（1）新增非

农就业大幅增加。10 月美国新增非农就业 16.5 万人，较上月明显回升，显示此前偏弱的就
业增长有所修复，企业招聘需求有所改善。同时，新增就业规模仍低于 8 月水平，劳动力市
场尚未恢复至此前较强的扩张状态。（2）失业率小幅回落，就业市场压力有所缓解。10 月失
业率由上月的 4.2%下降至 4.1%，新增失业人数由增加转为减少，反映劳动力市场压力边际
缓解。（3）私营部门为就业增长的主要支撑。10 月私人部门新增就业明显增加，贡献了大部
分新增非农就业，说明企业部门招聘需求有所恢复。总体看，美国新增就业回升，失业率下
降，就业市场明显改善，劳动力短期扩张动能有所恢复。
 结构：服务业支撑增强，制造业就业边际改善。
 私营服务业是新增就业的主要支撑，制造业就业有所回暖，但行业表现仍分化。（1）制造业
就业有所改善，耐用品制造业表现有韧性，是制造业就业增长的主要支撑，非耐用品制造业就
业则有所减少。（2）专业和商业服务、贸易运输等行业支撑服务业就业增长，就业保持较强
增势；信息业就业延续减少，金融活动就业小幅增加。（3）部分传统服务行业就业偏弱，教

育、住宿和餐饮以及艺术和娱乐等行业就业均有所减少。总体看，服务业就业增长主要由专业
和商业服务、贸易运输等行业带动，传统服务行业表现相对疲弱，制造业就业虽有所改善，但
整体扩张仍不均衡。
 工资：薪资同比增速小幅放缓，环比上涨压力仍存。
 美国私营部门平均时薪同比小幅回落，但环比增速有所回升，薪资上涨压力总体温和。（1）
薪资同比增速继续放缓。10 月美国私营非农部门平均时薪同比增长 3.1%，较上月小幅下降，
反映工资中长期上涨压力有所缓解。（2）薪资环比增速有所回升。10 月平均时薪环比上涨
0.4%，较上月有所提高，显示短期工资上涨仍具一定韧性。总体看，薪资同比增速放缓，但
环比涨幅回升，工资对服务价格和核心通胀的支撑作用仍需关注。
 政策：就业韧性支撑进一步加息，油价回落或放缓紧缩节奏。
 就业市场反弹叠加通胀高位，美联储进一步加息必要性仍存，但油价回落将缓解通胀压力，后
续加息节奏或趋于放缓。（1）就业市场改善，为美联储货币政策提供紧缩空间。新增非农就
业回升、失业率下降，反映美国劳动力市场仍具韧性，为维持偏紧货币政策提供了条件。（2）

通胀仍处高位，但油价回落有望缓解通胀压力。美国中期选举后，市场预期特朗普政府可能寻
求与伊朗达成协议，国际原油价格明显回落。若油价维持低位，能源价格对整体通胀的推升作
用将减弱。（3）进一步加息概率较高，但节奏趋于放缓。就业市场韧性与通胀压力支持进一
步加息，但油价回落或降低通胀上行风险，美联储后续加息或更加审慎，紧缩节奏趋缓。
 总结而言，10 月美国就业市场明显反弹，新增非农就业回升，失业率小幅下降，劳动力市场
韧性有所增强。结构上，服务业和耐用品制造业支撑就业增长，行业分化仍然明显；薪资同
比增长放缓，环比增速回升，整体薪资压力相对温和。往后看，就业韧性与通胀粘性仍支持
美联储维持紧缩立场，但国际油价回落有望缓解能源通胀压力，后续加息节奏或趋于放缓。
 风险提示：美国就业数据超预期，美国通胀水平超预期，地缘冲突风险。
证券分析师
陈国文

评级说明
投资建议的评级标准
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为股票评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内的相对市场表现，也即：
以报告发布日后的 6 个月内的公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅作为基准。其中：A 股市场：沪深 300
指数（000300.SH）；新三板市场：三板成指（899001.CSI）（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（899002.CSI）（针对做市转让标的）；
北交所市场：北证 50 指数（899050. BJ）；香港市场：恒生指数（HIS.HI）；美国市场：标普 500 指数（SPX.GI）或纳斯达克指数（IXIC.GI）。
股票评级
买入 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅大于 15%
增持 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 5%～15%之间
持有 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-5%～5%之间

卖出 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅小于-5%
行业评级
强于大市 相对表现优于同期相关证券市场代表性指数
中性 相对表现与同期相关证券市场代表性指数持平
弱于大市 相对表现弱于同期相关证券市场代表性指数

## Document 011: 10月通胀数据点评：能源链主导上游涨价，低位行业延续修复

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月通胀数据点评：能源链主导上游涨价，低位行业延续修复.pdf
Report source: 联储证券
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告 
宏观经济点评 2026 年 11 月 10 日

请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明

10 月通胀数据点评：能源链主导上游涨价，低位行业延续修复   
[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
0.7%，指向工业品价格仍处上行区间，上涨速度出现放缓。结构上看，一
是输入性能源价格继续强化，化工链价格上升。 10 月中东局势扰动仍然
较强，国际能源价格升至高位，输入性价格继续传导。石油和天然气开采
业价格同比升至 35.0%（上月 24.5%），石油煤炭加工业升至 29.9%（上
月 21.6%），化学原料制品制造业升至 16.4%（上月 12.1%），化纤制造业
16.0%（上月 12.3%）；环比看，石油和天然气开采业上涨 6.0%（上月
10.4%）。二是煤炭价格维持高增，上行斜率 放缓。煤炭开采和洗选业价
格同比升至 36.6%（上月 35.3%），环比涨幅降至 2.7%（上月 9.5%）。供
给端约束叠加高油价替代效应，煤价维持高位，但经历前期快速上涨后边
际动能有所放缓。三是，有色链由前期持续走强转为降温。 有色金属矿采
选业同比降至 16.3%（上月 21.4%），有色冶炼同比降至 18.6%（上月
21.4%）。AI 产业链下游价格维持韧性，计算机通信和其他电子设备制造
业同比由 5.8%小幅升至 5.9%，电气机械和器材制造业维持 6.2%。 
生产资料与生活资料 PPI 分化延续，部分长期负增行业继续收窄。10
月，生产资料 PPI 同比上涨 7.4%，较 9 月提高 0.9 个百分点；生活资料
PPI 同比下降 0.1%，与 9 月持平，二者分化仍然明显。细分看，一方
面，部分此前长期低迷行业延续修复 。一是建筑材料链延续改善。非金制
品业价格同比降幅收窄至 1.6%（上月 2.5%），环比涨幅扩大至 0.8%（上
月 0.3%）；黑金冶炼和压延加工业同比上涨 0.4%（上月-0.2%），指向建
筑材料链延续了 9 月以来修复趋势，或与“反内卷”以来相关行业产能调
整、供求关系改善有关。二是多数长期负增的消费制造行业同比降幅继续
收窄。汽车制造业由-2.0%收窄至-1.8%，医药制造业由-3.3%收窄至-
3.2%，酒饮料和精制茶制造业由-5.3%收窄至-5.2%，农副食品加工业由-
0.9%收窄至-0.4%。另一方面，下游价格改善仍未形成全面扩散。 生活资
料中，衣着、一般日用品价格降幅小幅扩大，耐用消费品同比涨幅回落至
1.4%（上月 1.6%）；环比看，生活资料整体增速为-0.1%（上月 0.1%）。 
往后看，上下游价格分化存在进一步收敛的条件， 广谱性修复仍需内需接
力。一方面，中期选举后美国国内对进一步扩大中东冲突的约束有所增
强，前期推动油价快速上行的地缘风险溢价或有所回落，能源价格继续大
幅上冲的概率下降，输入性通胀对 PPI 上游价格的边际支撑可能趋弱。另
一方面，建筑材料及部分低位制造业价格已连续改善，叠加四季度政策项
目逐步形成实物工作量，黑色、建材等行业仍存在进一步修复的条件 。后
续价格结构性分化有望从两端同时收敛，一端是能源等上游行业价格上行
斜率放缓，另一端是建筑链及部分长期低迷行业逐步修复。总量上看，10
月核心 CPI 和生活资料 PPI 均未同步走强，指向当前下游修复尚未形成广
泛的内生价格动能。后续判断价格向广谱改善转变仍在于两条链条，一是
黑色、建材等价格改善能否在政策需求支撑下延续并向中游制造扩散； 二
是食品加工、汽车、医药等低位行业跌幅收窄能否进一步转化为生活资料
PPI、核心 CPI 和服务价格的持续改善。

风险提示：国际油价大幅波动超预期、国内需求修复不及预期。

結論
➢ 結論
AI算力需求推升資料中心擴建，電力與散熱成為建置瓶頸
2030年AI Workload容量上看156GW(CAGR 28.8%)；電網補強需3~5年，機房建置僅需1~2年。
接電時程落差推升現地供電需求，SOFC取得大型AIDC訂單
Oracle、Nebius、AEP/Google相繼採用BE燃料電池；2030年SOFC市場機會約22GW，資料中心占49%。
液冷由晶片延伸至整機與電源，規格與價值量同步升級
Rubin世代全面液冷，CDU放大至2.3~3MW；800VDC電源櫃液冷化，快接頭升級至大口徑與ZQD。
看好液冷冷板、快接頭與SOFC關鍵零組件供應鏈
奇鋐(3017)、富世達(6805)受惠液冷規格升級；高力(8996)切入SOFC供應鏈，宏致(3605)列入觀察。

### Page 4

3
AI 算力需求推升科技業者資本支出，帶動資料中心長期擴建
➢ AI算力需求與AIDC建置瓶頸
預估全球AI Workload容量需求將由2025年44GW成長至2030年156GW，CAGR達28.8%，五年新增112GW。顯示AI算力
需求將持續帶動資料中心建置及相關基礎設施投資。
預估北美主要雲端與科技業者(CSP)資本支出將由2025年4,450億美元成長至2028年1.15兆美元，CAGR達37.2%。隨CSP持
續投入算力擴建，將帶動AI伺服器、機櫃、電力及散熱等基礎設施需求，支撐相關供應鏈中長期成長動能。
AI算力需求驅動資料中心擴建，2030年AI Workload容量上看156GW CSP資本支出加速擴張，2028年規模上看1.15兆美元
資料來源：McKinsey & Company、兆豐投顧整理
GW
資料來源：兆豐投顧預估及整理

### Page 5

4
AIDC 擴建推升用電規模與機櫃密度，供電及散熱能力影響建置進度
➢ AI算力需求與AIDC建置瓶頸
電網擴建與資料中心建置存在時程落差 ，電力取得速度成為AIDC擴張的重要限制。IEA指出，新建電網從規劃、許可至完工
可能需5–15年，資料中心建置則約1–3年。
AI運算朝高密度系統整合，配電與液冷升級需求涵蓋GPU及CSP自研ASIC。除NVIDIA GB200／GB300外，Google TPU 8t
／8i採第四代液冷，AWS Trainium3 Teton3 MAX採冷板液冷架構，AMD Helios亦已有支援最高246kW雙寬機櫃的合作參
考設計，系統驗證與量產交付能力將成為AIDC建置之關鍵。
全球再生能源與大型儲能併網排隊規模逾2,300 GW 高密度GPU與ASIC機櫃功率上升將持續推動配電及液冷升級
資料來源：IEA、兆豐投顧整理
GW
資料來源：兆豐投顧預估及彙整
kW

### Page 6

5
電網補強與機房建置存在時程落差，提升AIDC現地供電需求
➢ AI算力需求與AIDC建置瓶頸
AIDC擴張需同步取得發電供應、輸配電容量及場址受電能力，任一環節受限，皆可能影響機房投運時程。隨單一場址用電規
模擴大，部分專案需新增變電站 、饋線及相關設備，供電進度因而受到重電設備交期拉長 、區域電網併網與饋線容量不足 、
熱損耗與轉換效率等因素影響。
NLR指出，美國資料中心建置與負載爬坡通常約需12–24個月，公用事業電網補強則常需3–5年。我們認為，兩者的時程落差
將提高業者評估分階段供電及現地發電的意願 ，並為燃料電池與燃氣機組等方案創造導入機會；實際效益仍須綜合評估燃料 、
許可、可靠度與完整供電成本。
AIDC供電涉及多個環節，場址接電能力影響投運時程 資料中心(AIDC)建置與電網補強時程比較

### Page 7

6
接電時程落差推升現地供電需求，SOFC提供模組化連續供電選項
➢ SOFC現地供電與台廠供應鏈
當電網補強時程無法配合機房投運 ，業者可評估現地發電，搭配分階段送電與儲能等方式銜接供電需求 。方案選擇須綜合考
量連續供電能力、燃料供應、建置條件與可靠度要求。
Bloom Energy採用固態氧化物燃料電池(SOFC)，透過電化學反應將燃料轉換為電力，可依需求配置模組，提供持續性現地
電源。其應用可涵蓋與電網並聯或微電網架構，實際部署仍須配合燃料、許可及場內電力系統設計。
現地發電增加機房供電來源 現地發電與儲能方案比較
資料來源：Bloom Energy、兆豐投顧整理 資料來源：DOE、Cummins、Caterpillar、BE、兆豐投顧整理
註：本圖簡化供電路徑，電力轉換、UPS、儲能與備援配置依案場設計
方案 主要功能 選用時的評估重點
電池儲能 電力銜接 與功率調節 儲能量體、輸出功率及充電來源
柴油發電機 停電備援 依額定選擇運轉用途 燃料補給、設備額定及適用許可
燃氣渦輪 可配置為 持續發電來源 天然氣供應與工程、運轉條件

SOFC燃料電池 模組化配置 持續發電 燃料供應、高溫啟停及電堆維護
註：實際用途依額定與案場設計；天然氣SOFC仍有CO2排放

### Page 8

7
SOFC以電化學反應直接發電，模組化整合支援資料中心現地供電
➢ SOFC現地供電與台廠供應鏈
BE採固態氧化物燃料電池 (SOFC)，氧離子穿過固態電解質與燃料進行電化學反應直接發電 ，電子經外部迴路流動 ，形成直
流電，不經燃燒，電效率達 53–65%。SOFC由多片電池組成電堆 ，再整合為發電模組，搭配燃料處理、空氣供應、熱管理
與電力轉換設備，形成完整供電系統。
一座1GW的資料中心採用濕式冷卻燃氣渦輪機聯合循環發電系統 ，在其使用壽命期間，大約會消耗250億加侖的水；但若是
用燃料電池僅需140萬加侖。
氧離子穿越陶瓷電解質，電子經外部迴路形成電力 電池片逐級整合為電堆、發電模組與供電系統
資料來源：IEA、兆豐投顧整理 資料來源：AEP、兆豐投顧整理

### Page 9

8
發電效率與部署條件，決定SOFC在AIDC的採用機會
➢ SOFC現地供電與台廠供應鏈
AIDC持續運轉將累積大量燃料支出 ，提高發電效率可降低相同電力輸出所需的天然氣量 。SOFC具高效率發電特性，其經濟
價值須結合燃料價格、使用時數及維護成本評估。
SOFC、燃氣引擎與燃氣渦輪各有部署及運轉條件 。業主須共同評估設備交付 、場址工程、燃氣供應、排放許可及維運需求 ，
才能判斷適合的現地供電方案。
資料來源：兆豐投顧試算 資料來源：DOE、GE、Caterpillar、BE、兆豐投顧整理
相同電力輸出下，提高效率可降低燃料需求 現地供電方案須兼顧效率、擴建與運轉維護

### Page 10

9
政策支援燃料電池投資，木星計畫燃氣與許可牽動落地時程
➢ SOFC現地供電與台廠供應鏈
木星計畫位於美國新墨西哥州 ，為Oracle支援OpenAI Stargate建置的AI資料中心園區。Oracle於2026年4月宣布，規劃採
用最高2.45GW的Bloom Energy燃料電池裝置容量，取代原先燃氣輪機與柴油發電機方案，展現對SOFC現地供電的需求。
木星計畫的SOFC方案仍以天然氣供應為前提，燃氣接入與空氣品質許可均影響建置進度。州土地辦公室7月維持拒發特定管
線通行權等申請，Oracle於10月另提出微電網設計補件，顯示大型專案須同步協調設備建置、燃料供應與案場許可。
美國 OBBBA 將合格燃料電池納入 Section 48E 投資稅額抵減 甲骨文木星計劃微電網建置須經許可審查與方案調整
資料來源：GPO、兆豐投顧整理 資料來源：NMED、NMSLO、Oracle、兆豐投顧整理
項目 內容
適用制度 Section 48E 合格燃料電池專屬條款
抵減比例 合格投資基礎金額 x 30%

排放條件 專屬條款排除一般設施的溫室氣體排放資格門檻
加成限制 此項30%比例不得疊加其他加成

### Page 11

10
大型AIDC合作案擴大，BE交付與擴產節奏成為供應鏈觀察重點
➢ SOFC現地供電與台廠供應鏈
Oracle、Nebius等業者已公告採用BE燃料電池，顯示SOFC已取得大型AIDC商業合作。評估需求規模時，須分別辨識合作
上限、已簽約容量及實際部署進度。
機組製造、場址工程與許可程序須共同推進。供應鏈需求應依BE製造擴產、設備出貨及案場交付節奏追蹤，並將融資合作框
架與設備訂單分開評估。
BE大型合作案涵蓋設備採購、供電服務與專案融資 BE製造產能隨現地供電需求擴張，2027年起每年增加逾2GW

### Page 12

SOFC擴建帶動高溫熱管理與電力線纜需求，台廠切入不同供應環節
➢ SOFC現地供電與台廠供應鏈
依Ceres基於BNEF資料之分析 ，2030年全球SOFC市場機會約22GW，其中資料中心應用占 49%(約10.8GW)，為最大單一
應用市場。隨AI資料中心擴建與供電需求提升 ，資料中心可望成為SOFC商業化的重要需求來源 ，帶動高溫熱管理與電力零
組件的應用機會。
SOFC系統需要高溫熱交換 、電力轉換與系統配線等零組件 。高力提供熱反應器 、複熱器等高溫熱管理關鍵零組件；宏致則
供應高溫反應模組耐高溫電纜、電力控制與轉換模組內電纜，以及系統管理與電力傳輸線纜。
Ceres預估2030年全球SOFC市場機會達22GW，其中資料中心占49% 325 kW Energy Server 的組件與運作方式

### Page 13

12
供電與液冷協同升級，支撐AIDC算力密度與能源效率提升
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
在總供電及機房空間受限下，AIDC須兼顧能源與空間使用效率。降低冷卻與配電等基礎設施耗能，可在相同總用電條件下提
高IT設備用電占比；評估整體效益時，除PUE外，仍須觀察單位運算耗電及單位面積算力。
液冷透過冷板、分歧管與CDU將熱帶至設施端，高壓直流與Power sidecar則調整供電及設備配置。隨散熱需求延伸至電源
模組，供電與冷卻系統須協同設計 ，兼顧電力轉換、散熱耗能及部署密度。而電源模組採用液冷，全新的水冷資料中心機房
即不再需要傳統的冷熱通道(Hot/Cold Aisle)與大型空調系統，基礎設施CAPEX預估可節省高達10%。
相同總用電下，降低基礎設施耗能可提高IT用電占比 運算與電源同步液冷，降低對機房氣流散熱的依賴
資料來源：DOE、兆豐投顧試算 資料來源：NVIDIA、MEGMEET、兆豐投顧整理
度/電
註：依PUE定義進行情境試算，非特定液冷方案實測結果。IT設備用

電包含伺服器內部風扇等耗電，不等同晶片運算用電。

### Page 14

13
熱捕捉範圍擴大，液冷設計由主晶片延伸至整機架構
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
除CPU、GPU、TPU及ASIC等主要熱源外，液冷進一步涵蓋DIMM、OSFP光模組與板級元件，透過冷板及導熱板提高液體
帶走熱量的比例，降低對氣流散熱的依賴。
為支援不同熱源與機構配置 ，運算主板與整機伺服器之液冷解決方案多元化 ，包括冷板迴路式、混合式及整合式大冷板架構 。
我們認為，隨液冷涵蓋元件增加，流道與連接設計、模組組裝及可靠度驗證的重要性將提高 ，供應鏈機會亦由單顆冷板延伸
至整機冷卻整合。
液冷涵蓋主晶片與周邊元件，熱捕捉比例最高達100% 冷板迴路式、混合式與整合式架構支援不同整機配置
資料來源：CoolIT、兆豐投顧整理 資料來源： CoolIT、兆豐投顧整理

### Page 15

14
CDU串接IT與設施冷卻迴路，承接液冷擴張後的排熱需求
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
隨液冷由CPU、GPU延伸至記憶體、光模組及板級元件，液體承擔的設備熱量增加；機櫃功率密度提高 ，更進一步推升液冷
迴路的排熱需求。CDU串接IT側與設施側，透過熱交換器、循環泵浦與控制系統維持供液條件 ，其換熱能力與供液穩定性將
成為高密度AIDC的重要設計環節。
CDU升級除提高散熱容量，更須兼顧能源與空間使用效率 。OCP 2026的2.3MW系統模擬研究顯示，在相同散熱負載下，比
較方案間全年冷卻系統能耗差距約17%。
冷板取熱、CDU換熱、設施端排熱，構成完整液冷迴路 相同2.3MW散熱負載之下，CDU選型仍牽動流量與全年能耗

註：典型液對液CDU示意圖。紅、藍代表各迴路相對高低溫，非特定
案場溫度。

比較項目 OCP研究結果
散熱負載 各方案均為2.3MW
設施側回水溫度 約34~39°C
設施側流量需求 約660~940 GPM

差距，並非液冷普遍節電率。2.3MW為散熱負載，非設備耗電量。

Deschutes開放2MW級CDU設計，瞄準能源與空間效率提升
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
Google將第五代 CDU設計Deschutes貢獻予OCP，提供高密度 AI機房可採用的開放規格 。其v1.0設計以2MW散熱容量 、
3°C接近溫差及IT側與設施側各500GPM流量為目標，透過三組雙流程熱交換器並聯，兼顧換熱表現與流阻，支撐高熱負載液
冷部署。依規格本體尺寸換算 ，Deschutes占地約1.78平方公尺，散熱容量密度約 1.12MW／平方公尺；搭配 N+1無軸封泵
浦及溫度、壓力與流量監控，兼顧容量與供液可靠度。我們認為，CDU的競爭重點將涵蓋單位占地容量 、低溫差換熱及泵浦
耗能，須從整體冷卻系統評估其能源與空間效益。
熱交換器、泵浦與控制整合等關鍵組件，支撐MW級CDU穩定運轉 2MW容量與3°C接近溫差，兼顧高熱負載與緊湊配置

800VDC重整供電配置，液冷延伸至高密度電源櫃
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
隨AIDC機櫃功率提高，低電壓供電面臨大電流、導體體積與電源設備占用空間等挑戰。Power sidecar將前端電力轉換與部
分備援設備移至獨立電源櫃 ，再以800VDC送至運算櫃，並依架構於負載附近降壓；透過提高輸電電壓與調整設備配置 ，支
援更高功率密度部署。
NVIDIA第二代MGX Power Rack參考設計，朝100%液冷發展，並標示880kW供電能力(N配置、含BBU)及90秒備援供電時
間。隨電力轉換與備援設備集中，液冷需求將進一步延伸至電源模組，使供電與散熱成為能源及空間效率升級的重要環節。
前端電源移至獨立機櫃，800VDC支援高功率密度供電 NVIDIA MGX第二代電源櫃朝全液冷發展，液冷散熱模組與零組件用量提升

### Page 18

17
液冷導入電源模組，推進功率密度與能源效率升級
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
電源模組導入液冷後，可透過冷板將功率元件的熱量帶至冷卻液 ，降低對風扇、大型散熱鰭片與風道的需求 。相較風冷設計，
液冷有助縮小散熱結構占用空間，並降低風扇耗電及元件工作溫度，使電源模組朝更高功率密度發展。
MEGMEET於2026年10月OCP展示100kW、800V液冷AC/DC電源層，採2RU不含BBU設計，產品標示轉換效率逾98%；另
展示1RU、95kW的800V轉54V液冷DC/DC電源層。我們認為，液冷應用延伸至整流與降壓環節 ，將擴大電源冷板、流道設
計及模組整合的需求，提升水冷板、快接頭、滑軌、機殼與液冷busbar之需求量。
水冷板承接功率元件熱量，降低電源模組對風扇與風道的依賴 液冷電源層涵蓋前端整流與櫃內降壓，支援緊湊供電配置

晶片TDP與出貨量同步提升，水冷板技術與ASP仍具升級空間
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
晶片TDP逐代提升，NVIDIA由GB300的1.4kW增至Rubin 2.3kW、Rubin Ultra約2.6kW，TPU v9與Trainium4將達3~4kW。
我們認為，至少至Rubin Ultra世代，3kW以內熱負載仍可由目前冷板架構解熱 ，並透過微通道、雙面水冷板(4→6kW)及鑽
石銅等材料升級持續解熱。
我們預估AI晶片出貨量由2025年1,036萬顆增至2027年2,403萬顆(26/27年YoY+39%/+67%)，其中ASIC於2027年達1,333萬
顆、占比由37%升至55%，超越GPU；出貨放量加上電源櫃與光模組導入液冷，水冷板用量與滲透率仍有提升空間。
TDP持續攀升，水冷板技術與ASP仍有升級空間 AI晶片出貨量預估，整體供應鏈於2027年仍供不應求

冷板與流體零組件升級，須兼顧散熱效能與系統耗電
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
晶片功耗提升推高冷板流量需求 ，惟CDU可供IT側使用的壓差有限(Deschutes規格為80~90psi)，冷板與快接頭須以更低壓
降輸送更大流量。Rubin Ultra將導入內建球閥、提升流量的ZQD；我們認為，快接頭技術門檻由防漏延伸至大流量低壓降，
帶動規格與單價升級。
NVIDIA MGX第二代電源櫃全面液冷，以1座電源櫃搭配2座運算櫃為一組試算，每組新增26片電源冷板及52顆快接頭；惟兩
段電源轉換熱損合計僅約供電量4%，增量以件數為主，價值量約為運算櫃快接頭的5%。
電源設備液冷化：每組(1*電源櫃+2*運算櫃)零組件用量試算 流量提升推動快接頭規格升級，Rubin Ultra每tray 10顆MQD改採ZQD

每櫃液冷價值隨世代提升，水冷板與快接頭量價齊揚

液冷由晶片延伸至整機與電源，單櫃價值倍增帶動台廠受惠
➢ 液冷系統擴張與AIDC效率升級
液冷由CPU、GPU延伸至記憶體、光模組及電源設備，Rubin世代全面採用100%液冷，CDU容量放大至2.3~3MW。我們預
估，單櫃液冷價值(含CDU)由約10萬美元倍增至約20萬美元，全球液冷市場將由2026年105億美元成長至2030年853億美元
(CAGR約69%)。
價值升級沿三條主線：CDU放大容量與泵浦冗餘、冷板導入雙面水冷與鑽石銅、快接頭朝大口徑低壓降及ZQD升級；電源櫃
液冷則以件數貢獻增量。我們認為，冷板與快接頭兼具規格升級與用量成長，奇鋐、雙鴻、富世達最直接受惠。
全球液冷市場2030年達853億美元，二次側占約7成5 液冷各環節升級方向與單櫃價值量占比

### Page 23

22
結論
➢ 結論
AI算力需求推升資料中心擴建，電力與散熱成為建置瓶頸
2030年AI Workload容量上看156GW(CAGR 28.8%)；電網補強需3~5年，機房建置僅需1~2年。
接電時程落差推升現地供電需求，SOFC取得大型AIDC訂單
Oracle、Nebius、AEP/Google相繼採用BE燃料電池；2030年SOFC市場機會約22GW，資料中心占49%。
液冷由晶片延伸至整機與電源，規格與價值量同步升級
Rubin世代全面液冷，CDU放大至2.3~3MW；800VDC電源櫃液冷化，快接頭升級至大口徑與ZQD。
看好液冷冷板、快接頭與SOFC關鍵零組件供應鏈
奇鋐(3017)、富世達(6805)受惠液冷規格升級；高力(8996)切入SOFC供應鏈，宏致(3605)列入觀察。

## Document 090: 2026年7-9月期の実質GDP～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期の実質GDP～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

（前期比、％）
（年・四半期）
実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移
民間最終消費支出 民間企業設備 その他民需 公需 外需
（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
( )内は年率換算値
(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

2026年度

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として 輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％） 、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％） 、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％） 、うち中国向けが

Bull/Bear: Globalwafers Co (6488.TWO)
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 6

Globalwafers Co (6488.TWO)
04 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Globalwafers Co
Company description
GWC is the world's third-largest silicon wafer manufacturer. The company
offers a one-stop shop for customers with its wide range of product portfolio
and global presence. GWC has 16 manufacturing sites in 10 countries spread
across Asia, Europe, and the Americas. Its product offering include annealed,
polished, non-polished, diffused, FZ, EPI, SOI wafers ranging from 4” to 12” in

diameter.
Investment strategy
We rate GWC as Buy as we expect its sales and earnings to recover in 2027.
We believe silicon wafer sector has hit the bottom in 2Q26, evidenced by UTR
improvements across 6” to 12” wafers. Although GM would likely face short-
term headwinds in 2H26 due to rising depreciation burden, a fire incident and
higher energy costs, we believe favorable pricing trend, full UTR and the
reception of US government’s subsidies would help offset the negative

impact, especially in 2027.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$920 is based on 28x 2H27-1H28E EPS, in line with
+1stdv of 1-year fwd PE in the past 5 years. We use this PE multiple to reflect a
gradually improving demand outlook for the overall semiconductor industry
from 2H26 onward, especially a more meaningful recovery in 2027 and likely
shortage. We believe the upcoming megatrend demand from AI, HPC, auto,
and Io T and supply tightness could justify GWC's valuations during recovery

backdrop from 1Q27 onwards.
Risks
Key downside risks that could cause the GWC shares to trade below our
target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected pull-in demand; 2)
undisciplined capacity expansion by other industry participants; 3) wafer
price decline driven by macro factors; and 4) late reception of the subsidies
from the US government.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Earnings revision:  
We revise up our 2026 earnings estimates by 1% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
down our 2026E revenue estimates by 18%). We revise up 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 14%/23% to reflect a better product mix with higher contribution from the server 
business (we revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 2/4%). We now expect the 
GM expansion to be faster due to the higher server business contribution, and thus 
revise up GPM by 5.3/3.1/4.4ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Valuation:  
We reiterate our Buy rating on Fositek with a new 12m TP of NT$2,390 (from NT$2,082), 
based on 22.6 2027E P/E (unchanged), which is the company’s average P/E since 2022, 
and factoring in our earnings revision.

### Page 3

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Fositek is a global leading hinges supplier, mainly for foldable smartphones (56% of 
2025 revenues). We expect the company’s foldable smartphone hinges revenues to 
show solid growth in 2026/27E driven by (1) rising foldable phone market penetration 
rate; (2) expanding customer base, from China smartphones to more global-tier 
smartphones; and (3) stabilized smartphone market demand, encouraging brand makers 
to launch new form factors to attract consumers. Competitive edges of Fositek lie in its 
strong innovation (e.g., hinges for rollable phones, wearable phones), in-house molding 
and automated production to secure precision, long experience to support fast delivery, 
and leading customer base with expansion (e.g., supplying Huawei since the first 
foldable phone back in 2019).  We continue to see Fositek successfully entering the 
AI-related business, especially QD (quick disconnect) products for AI server liquid 
cooling. The company has the largest QD production capacity (4.5mn units/month) 
among Taiwanese thermal suppliers and will continue to expand if demand continues to 
accelerate. Furthermore, the company continues to make progress with its rail kit 
business, which will continue to break into AI servers (including both GPU servers and 
ASIC servers). Overall, we believe Fositek will successfully transition from a 
consumer-focused company to AI-focused company, with server-related business to 
contribute 94% of total revenue in 2028E (vs. 50% in 2025), and will see GM continue to 
expand to 37.2% in 2028E (vs. 26.3% in 2025).  
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Fositek 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$2,390 is based on a target 22.6x 2027 P/E, 
which is the company’s average P/E since 2022. We consider this multiple appropriate 
given the increasing contribution from AI-related products (including QD and rail kits) 
could offset the potential weakness from its foldable smartphone business. 
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected contribution from AI-related products, 
(2) slower-than-expected foldable phone penetration; and (3) Potential competition 
from more suppliers

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.

Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers
n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity
Nan Ya PCB (8046.TW)

[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and

n Better-than-expected ABF and BT demand
n Faster-than-expected pickup in AI and 5G demand

n Stronger-than-expected ASP hike
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall, creating headwinds for ABF substrate demand and pricing
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr

## Document 287: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26_ 조선사는 우직해야 한다HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26_ 조선사는 우직해야 한다3Q26_ 조선사는 우직해야 한다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26_ 조선사는 우직해야 한다HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26_ 조선사는 우직해야 한다3Q26_ 조선사는 우직해야 한다 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180 억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK 을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.  
Buy (Maintain)

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation

NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
No
vember 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015, 1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries, 2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks. 4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index. 5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

4 Institute of International Finance, as of September 23, 2026.
5 FactSet, as of October 27, 2026.

MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.
Exhibit 4: Market Leadership Has Been Shifting Beneath the Surface.
A) October Saw a Sharp Broadening in Market Leadership. B) Value Has Quietly Led Growth in 2026.
